STRING/list and atom-encoding duplicate keys; integer1 and float1.0 differ.

==, encoded-key bytes or a string-key map for this contract.

to invoke decoded foreign data or a memory-safety theorem:

### Logical source bounds and real read responsibilities

For H events, at most3H references are examined in payload/result/error order;
fetch deduplication keeps first occurrence before filtering binary, nonempty
refs at65535 bytes. ValueStore separately recognizes actual p/r/e/s/n key grammar,
including canonical version/name forms. A length-valid non-flow ref receives a
missing slot. No payload read is justified merely by accepting arbitrary bytes
as a reference, and the neighboring4096 option bound is not this path's bound.
Event IDs, unrelated fields, event order and existing flags remain intact.
At most six field keys per event are added; errors/omission/missing branches do
not clear older values, errors or flags. Stored byte count includes the FSV2
wrapper; type1 strips one header only, type2 decodes safe ETF, and unknown or
incomplete wrappers stay raw. Compressed top-level ETF is rejected before the
BIF; malformed/trailing typed data becomes the exact invalid-flow field error.
Allocation/resource failure must not be silently translated into corrupt data.

A real reader must preserve each requested position and meaningful failure
provenance. Only eligible PRIMARY nils trigger fallback; storage failure does
not. Actual source fallback routes by reference, reads real LMDB/cold/history/
WARaft locators and materializes blobs. Some fallback decode/read failures become
nil by source design. A primary read failure remains a sanitized storage-error
field, not missing. File-ref omission uses validated stored size without payload
materialization; hot bytes and LMDB fallback may already materialize an oversized
value before HistoryValues omits it. max_bytes therefore limits returned stored
values, not all I/O, retained owners, batch memory or decoded graph size.

I verified the source's cardinality asymmetry directly. All-eligible replies
bypass fallback on count mismatch and later zip-truncate; mixed eligibility
restoration converts mismatched lists to all nil slots. Non-list results are not
automatically synthesized failures. Production Rust ports should guarantee exact
ordered slots; an independently source-shaped collector must preserve these
branches until a specific compatibility decision changes them. Router's physical
location/retry machinery need not be copied into redb, but real availability,
error/size, tenant/group/recovery, retention and reopen/snapshot guarantees do.
A coherent local read is not a quorum read barrier or projection watermark.

### Concrete next ownership and implementation decision

Existing Rust blob/query/projection files are reservations, and genuine state
point reads expose core record/policy/control/outcome data, not a raw value
catalog. Reduced FWSH history lacks arbitrary fields and payload/result/error
references. Neither it nor the private closed-policy ETF/FWPV representation is
complete successful hydration. The latter's depth64/span4096 profile must not
silently become generic source invalidity.

I support ARCH's next named codec consultation at existing value.rs and proposed
value/original_flow responsibility children, before the later actual ports/blob,
storage/blob and application history orchestration selections. The proposed
boundary moves Vec<u8> into an OriginalValueDocument and accepts a borrowed
explicit OriginalValueContext plus a separate resource profile. It returns a
flat typed node/edge document whose byte spans address its own moved buffers.
Views borrow that document. It must not return slices into temporary owned read
Vecs, use a self-referential struct, clone every leaf, or recursively own an
unbounded ETF tree whose parse/equality/drop can exhaust the native stack.
Exact names/fields/methods/private workers are proposals until ARCH records them.

Before final arenas are filled, syntax/count validation must check offsets,
length/arity multiplication, actual nodes/edges and needed work-stack slots,
including STRING expansion and arbitrary-precision magnitude storage. Counting
itself needs prospectively bounded/fallible scratch; there is no free recursive
preflight. Then reserve the actual selected owner arrays fallibly and fill using
IDs/ranges. Keep invalid wire, unavailable local profile/context, explicit
operational limits and real TryReserveError distinct. No arbitrary input/depth/
ref cap is introduced as source corruption. Adopted limits are honest operational
profiles, and unsupported profiles remain incomplete in the parity ledger.

Semantic map uniqueness is an additional real workload. Normalize equivalent
integer, atom and list spellings; preserve finite float bits and compare compound
keys structurally, including order-independent maps. A linear byte scanner does
not prove linear duplicate detection. A naive all-pairs key scan can be quadratic,
and repeated compound comparisons add traversal work. ARCH must select the exact
comparison/indexing and checked work accounting before claiming complexity.
Encoded owners, count/stack scratch and result arenas have separate live ranges;
drop retired scratch when possible. Moving one backing buffer avoids a whole
payload clone but may retain unused bytes/capacity until the document dies.
Stable fetch dedup is not permission to change per-occurrence source decoding or
cache across changing safe profiles.

Fallible Vec reservation is a suitable mechanism for these known counts, not a
whole allocator guarantee. I fetched Rust1.99's actual alloc/vec/mod.rs1415..1578,
blob21d88f9f2042b52ae6be25d048605eddfecdd08a: try_reserve_exact reports capacity/
allocator failures and does not deliberately grow geometrically, but the
allocator may grant excess capacity. Reserve before pushes and propagate the
real error; do not assert capacity==requested or automatically convert to Box:
[pinned Rust Vec source](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/blob/1.99.0/library/alloc/src/vec/mod.rs#L1530).
No external decoder crate is recommended/selected. Any such decision still needs
actual version, license and security review plus architecture approval.

The bounded validation plan is source golden/differential coverage for every
common successful type and alias, exact EOF/compression/unsafe context negatives,
truncation/length corruption, deep/wide operational refusal, and document views
after temporary read containers/context descriptors drop. Hydration then needs
all original three/four observations unchanged plus threshold/overlay/ref/mask/
cardinality tests and genuine persisted/reopened routed reads, nil-only fallback,
storage failures and validated omission metadata. Real publication must precede
accepted references and survive snapshot/recovery/retention. No callback success
or error-only helper replaces that path. Any new fuzz/benchmark/dependency fixture
requires its own named scope. No allocator fault branch, total CPU/heap/RSS,
measured gain, public admission, Linux or full workflow parity is claimed.
Native old8, initialR/fullE-T and same-ref settlement remain separate open work.

## UNAPPROVED CODEC BODY algorithm and resource proposal during R0 authorship

and32803+3601/00dadab44370050a0af0ffc59f9ffd7c26143ed04c2a8c9929d5abe42e2046ec.
The latter's PRIVATE document and five checked views supersede public-document
wording. Both complete stored len and incoming Vec<u8> capacity count toward
max_encoded_bytes; the decoder neither shrinks nor copies the input to evade
that owner profile. Root has also explicitly selected ENCODED typed-subterm
depth, root1/each child+1, INCLUDING zero-head LIST alias wrappers. That relay

### Minimum iterative producer

Use one checked grammar driver in census and build modes, with a single
monotonically spent work account covering BOTH passes, context lookups and final
semantic-key validation. Do not maintain two subtly different byte grammars or
reset the budget at a phase boundary. Initial profile validation/input-owner
checks precede scratch/result allocation. Raw, unknown/incomplete FSV2 and escape1
branches need one binary node and no context lookup; a typed branch requires
version/exact EOF and the selected source tag rules. All error offsets remain
relative to the complete stored input.

Census retains only a flat continuation stack, one active sibling position per
compound rather than enqueuing every future child. Before each frame push check
encoded depth, checked counts/work and prospective scratch growth; propagate a
real failed reservation. Aliases still count wire depth. Count the ACTUAL model
nodes/edges, including STRING-expanded integer heads and list tail, normalized
zero-head aliases, alternating map key/value IDs and foreign free-variable
edges. Foreign ref words and leaf bytes stay ranges in the input owner. Do not
preallocate a maximum deployment limit merely because it is large; use actual
counts and checked arithmetic. An enormous valid profile is not itself an
instruction to allocate it.

After census, perform one checked fallible exact reservation for the node Vec
and one for the edge Vec. Reuse or release census scratch deliberately. Build
with the same grammar, allocate each compound's contiguous edge slots and fill
node IDs through explicit continuations. Completion checks must establish every
slot is filled, exact counted totals, one root and exact EOF before returning.
Build never inserts encoded backreferences into a recursive Rust owner graph.
The private document owns the moved bytes and flat spines; dropping it remains
flat Vec destruction. No leaf String/Vec copy, recursive owned tree, public
constructor, new output wrapper or encoder is implied.

Syntax/safe/numeric checks possible during census should happen there. Semantic
duplicate maps require built typed keys; allocation may therefore already have
occurred when that validation refuses. This is not a promise that EVERY invalid
input can be rejected with no scratch or final arena allocation. Partial owners
stay private and are dropped on error. There is no partial successful document
or storage authority.

### Exact keys without a new hashing/interner subsystem

For the minimum BODY I recommend an iterative same_term evaluator with flat
continuation frames, and pairwise duplicate-key lookup per map. The earlier
canonical-rank/hash optimization is deferred. No additional interning model or
collision theorem is needed. Validate nested maps before their containing maps,
using the builder's acyclic ordering or an admitted explicit postorder walk;
keep the document's original pair ordering unchanged.

An equality frame retains sequence progress or ONE map candidate at a time and
pushes one actual child comparison. It never invokes itself recursively or
materializes a normalized key tree. Map equality matches each left key in the
right map and then its value, independent of pair ordering. With both maps already
unique there is at most one matching candidate. Every unsuccessful candidate and
every revisited nested term/byte consumes work. This can be quadratic or worse
under nested structural comparison; max_work_units is its explicit operational
stop, not proof of linear total CPU.

A list cursor walks each node's heads and then its tail, crossing segmented
LIST/STRING aliases without constructing a new list. Mathematical integers unify
small/signed/padded/large encodings; zero has no negative sign and any nonzero
source sign byte is negative. Latin1/UTF8 atoms compare as Unicode scalar names
without constructing a normalized String. Integer/float are distinct; finite
float bits preserve BOTH zero signs. Binary/bitstring comparison uses logical
bit length/content, including unused-bit normalization where required by the
selected source family. Foreign identity normalization must follow its actual
source fields/context, not encoded-byte equality or claimed execution. Ordinary
Atom nil and EmptyList remain distinct. No Raw::Other, FWPV or metadata-scalar
projection substitutes for a type.

### Proposed prospective work and reservation tariff

Before BODY, ARCH must record an exact tariff matching the implementation. My
concrete minimum proposal uses one unit per consumed wire byte in each pass,
plus one per produced/visited node or edge, continuation visit/push/pop, map
candidate and context row. Additional normalization/equality inspections charge
one per examined byte on EVERY visit, including UTF8/Latin1 comparison and
high-zero scans. Charging a consumed opaque span is a declared logical parser
handling tariff; it is not a claim that the CPU physically reread every byte.
Deduct before the operation/loop span; checked overflow or exhausted work returns
Limit. A raw constant-size jump is not evidence that subsequent key/UTF8 work is
free. Native Meter R/F, elapsed CPU and whole allocator/GC/RSS are separate.

Scratch reservations themselves need a precise growth policy. Grow only when
needed, using checked bounded batches rather than blind reserve_exact(1) at
every nesting step. Charge the reached live operations and a conservative
old-capacity*size_of(frame) relocation allowance BEFORE a fallible growth call;
check that product before calling the allocator. Frame/equality task counts
remain bounded by reached depth and available work, and only one candidate's
continuations are live. This makes physical-capacity-dependent operational
headroom explicit; it is not a portable exact CPU tariff. If ARCH instead selects
a deterministic logical-copy tariff, allocator excess/copy latency must remain
explicitly outside that bound. Do not silently claim both properties. Requested
scratch/result counts are bounded, while allocator overprovision can still make
capacity exceed the request. No capacity==requested, boxed-slice shrink or
allocator-failure harness is selected.

Context rows are borrowed captures, not inferred from the untrusted value.
None is unavailable; Some-known-absent is the source safe refusal. Reached table
validation/lookup must be charged, including compared name bytes and any
coherence/duplicate scan. The six-model error roster has no InvalidContext:
either coherent capture remains an explicit ingress precondition, or ARCH must
state that malformed/incoherent supplied capture yields UnsupportedContext at
the reached tag. It must not manufacture a known-absent atom from unusable
context. Unused context need not be traversed for a raw binary. Separate known
unsupported LOCAL/native-index profiles from malformed data and from real
TryReserveError. No returned context lifetime or BEAM lease is introduced.

### Pinned legacy float mechanics and the remaining boundary

I located and fetched the ACTUAL official Unix source online:
[OTP28.3.1 Unix sys_float.c](https://github.com/erlang/otp/blob/aa5b1d3366762ff8f420f2aa6a52604b481530c9/erts/emulator/sys/unix/sys_float.c#L73),
blob911464c25a106eefe9ee58a86c9d60219111178c. Its complete3720 bytes match the
local pinned source at /private/tmp/ferricstore2-policy-runtime-capture-otp-28.3.1/src/erts/emulator/sys/unix/sys_float.c
(SHA2567dc6c4402691788d9ec4590443c670c26c05e9b2f86566da858cb6e4bbabea30).
The earlier attempted common/sys_float path was wrong; it supplied no evidence.
I now read the complete actual file, not only a helper declaration.

Its syntax is optional sign, one-or-more ASCII digits, dot OR comma,
one-or-more digits, optional COMPLETE e/E exponent with optional sign and
one-or-more digits, then NUL. Whitespace and other suffix bytes before NUL
refuse. Conversion uses strtod, finite checks and a radix-character retry;
overflow refuses, while complete underflow to zero is accepted. These source
facts match Root's26 observations. The grammar is not ScoreBound's scanner or
unchecked Rust f64 FromStr. A selected Rust scalar helper can validate this
bounded prefix, normalize comma in at most31 local bytes, borrow checked ASCII
as str and perform checked finite conversion. It must not modify the owned
encoded buffer, whose public view promises unchanged bytes. A fixed local
buffer creates no whole-stack/allocator or libc/FENV/Linux equivalence theorem.

One precise operand remains: a FLOAT_EXT field with NO NUL among its31 bytes.
external.c delegates to a NUL-oriented helper then advances31; current goldens
do not settle that boundary. Before claiming total bounded behavior, Root may
use its existing inline wrapper after authorSTOP for (a) root field '1.' plus
29 zero bytes, and (b) the same field followed by a SMALL_INTEGER sibling in a
tuple. No new file/module/probe is proposed. Without that closure, select an
honest UnsupportedProfile case rather than inventing InvalidFlowValue or
assuming zero padding/full-field UTF8. Numeric tie/range portability remains
conditional on the actual selected conversion contract and separate OS evidence.

This proposal needs no R0 source change, test weakening, new public API or
workload. After genuine saved R0, ARCH can select exact private frame/helpers,
source subdivisions and the stated profile/error/work rules. Root then verifies
actual source/compiled geometry and unchanged-oracle target outcomes. The ten
supplemental codec cases do not map the original three HistoryValues cases;
real catalog/publication/routed reads/full hydration and old8/Linux remain open.

No packing, waiver, new error/class or public callback is introduced.

The new function is an honest ALWAYS-InvalidFlowValue baseline; it performs no
wrapper/ETF parse, context lookup, limit enforcement or output-arena allocation.
It consumes and drops the incoming Vec on failure. Therefore this source is not
an implemented resource gate or a passing old decoder. The six models match the
selected data contract: private encoded/node/edge/root owners; flat range/ID
nodes; finite float bits; arbitrary-width borrowed magnitude ranges; explicit
foreign identity DATA; optional captured tables; five positive resource axes;
source/context/profile/limit/actual-reservation error lanes. Debug-only document,
nodes and error avoid an implied payload Clone/Eq implementation. Copy context/
limits/function metadata do not own or execute a foreign runtime resource.

All five document views use ordinary checked slice access; bad IDs/ranges return
None. No public constructor, mutable view or context lifetime escapes. The model
has no recursively owned child graph. Limits explicitly include full stored
len AND moved input capacity, encoded depth including LIST0 wrappers, actual
node/edge spines and admitted work. Their existence is not enforcement by R0,
allocator-overprovision coverage, public opening R or storage availability.
No capacity==requested, automatic Box conversion, shrink or fake OOM is claimed.

The test oracles are meaningful and source-derived. Wrapper controls distinguish
raw/empty/incomplete/unknown magic from one-level escape and typed corruption.
Integer cases cover truthy sign2/255, empty/padded zero, i64 endpoints and a
nonzero20-byte magnitude with padding. Float expectations use literal binary64
bits, comma/NUL grammar and nonfinite/malformed refusal. The184-byte compound is
byte-for-byte equal to the actual original compound in packet8722488+11999;
its SHA256 is87d0e580ac0b3f9ba92c6e3dc696671b3b4a479f09aca4bfed342f20566df5fa.
The helper checks exact16-ID cardinality BEFORE indexing and all eight typed
values; nil atom, empty list, empty tuple and STRING/list cases remain distinct.

Each duplicate-map alias is first shown valid alone, then both pair orders must
refuse; this prevents an all-invalid decoder from satisfying the negative oracle.
Nested unordered-map, segmented/zero-head list, integer and Latin1/UTF8 atom

### What already exists and what is genuinely missing

I read stable WorkflowRecord64..145 and actual record encoding through180;
inline payload/result/error are ALREADY persisted in FWSR, not an unimplemented
blob abstraction. The actual ports/log.rs145..221 DecodedRequest owns represented
commands and borrows its encoded donor; value_bytes(index) borrows those existing
value owners without another copy. Publication::write_record and record_size
serialize all current inline fields. A catalog must preserve historical/generated/
shared reference availability; copying every unchanged current field into a new
value row on every transition would add gratuitous ownership and wrong versions.
Current reduced FWSH encodes event metadata but lacks those reference snapshots.
ActiveValueRef explicitly supplies logical identity without stored availability.
Ports/blob.rs and storage/blob.rs remain three-line reservations.

Fresh exact stable read identities include transaction358/f89b072d, store295/
7c0c6bb3, commit233/5788bd4d, commit/independent79/20d99c22, publication296/
1753832e, read/point211/63786911, key295/d221820c and physical106/fd179712.
These complete files were read except the broader workflow/log/record modules,
whose relevant ranges above define this review. I also read complete claim
commit138, claim trait1..125, recovery/rows1..160, recovery245..273 and current
snapshot format1..125/165..230 and validation1..140. Full-file hashes do not
claim unrelated source scopes were re-audited.

The exact earliest data deficit is explicit accepted per-item value/ref intent.
Original flow_create652..663 generates a NEW versioned reference on field
PRESENCE, even if the supplied bytes equal the old bytes; otherwise explicit or
old refs survive. flow_values323..345 writes only supplied fields. Therefore
comparing before/after bytes cannot recover the required intent. Actual log.rs
201..219 is positional: Cancel slot0 is ERROR; Complete/Fail slot1 is result/error;
Retry slot1 is error. fresh::stage_values167..185 FLATTENS Option slots. Its
flattened index cannot establish kind, and an absent payload can shift positions.
Join the actual Operation, explicit supplied-kind/presence and matching accepted
postimage/version. The proposed private publication attachment owns bounded
indices/descriptors, not a second payload Vec or a self-borrow into its owner.

Independent commit writes each final record once but EVERY successful history
plan. Catalog bytes/current refs/history refs must follow that full per-item
stream, including repeated same-ID intermediate values, not final_indices alone.
Claim has its own consumed commit_claim path and must carry refs into each real
claim history too. Genuine no-op/replay/rejection must create no new value row.
The single existing redb transaction already validates predecessor/control,
writes core/index/history/outcome/control, uses Immediate commit and fences
uncertainty. Extend that authority; do not publish to a second database first or
return a successful reference before the atomic commit.

### Concrete next responsibility cut, still unapproved

I support the candidate ports/blob value/reference/publication responsibility,
source-neutral stored-byte row plus independent expiry, profiled current/history
bindings and authentic ordered reader on the actual state owner. Existing
log/Fresh/independent/public-mutation/atomic-start/claim preparation must provide
and validate the attachment; storage key/value/redb/commit/read must consume it.
The first bounded generated nonterminal binary route can be real progress, with
legacy, typed/preencoded, terminal-retention, shared/named/external and public
routing gates EXPLICIT. It is not full HistoryValues or the original three maps.
The row schema must not hard-wire Binary as its only possible future value type.

Existing command Vec bytes are LOGICAL BINARY, not trusted stored ETF. Publication
must preserve source Support's escape rule: prepend FSV2+tag1 only when logical
bytes start FSV2, otherwise preserve them. A typed/preencoded discriminator,
intent/log version and full value encoder need their own later contract; never
reinterpret an existing Vec merely because its prefix resembles type2. Catalog
stored_len includes that source wrapper, while record payload bytes keep their
current semantics. None and Some(empty) differ. Do not replace core inline
fields with refs or Box as an incidental optimization.

Current source flow_values103..107 gives no expiry absent positive captured
terminal retention;414..425 explicitly leaves OLD terminal refs' TTL untouched.
New terminal values need the authentic captured expiry witness, which the
reduced durable core does not yet fully represent. Do not derive it from a later
clock/policy or cascade-delete old unexpired catalog values when record/history
retention runs. Shared/named linkage, ownership and cleanup remain real required
producer/lifecycle work, not an arbitrary opaque-key seeding fixture.

I verified current snapshot behavior, not the older summary: FWSS6 selects FWSP3;
FWSS5 selects FWSP2. Physical families remain0..7, and recovery rejects unknown
families. New catalog family/record/history envelopes require joint layout,
semantic row/relation/inventory, snapshot/cohort/receive/install/restore and
replay changes. A header/range edit alone is insufficient. On a value-enabled
layout, legacy plans cannot silently bypass ref/history maintenance. Replay or
backfill from real retained evidence, or explicitly refuse unsupported migration;
current inline bytes cannot reconstruct discarded historical values. Existing
snapshot cohorts and log-purge protection must retain the values they need.
Proposed family8/new layout are only reserved architecture candidates here.

### Ordered read ownership, costs and one concrete optimization candidate

A batch must have a slot for EVERY requested key, including duplicates/missing,
and may keep unique owned encoded buffers indexed by aligned slots. Use actual
scope/placement and one stable revision/control watermark; group-local coherence
is not a quorum barrier or proof of cross-group routing. Original routing uses
captured slot assignment after crc32&1023 over the real flow hash tag, not a
random HashMap or a caller's invented partition. Invalid source ref grammar is
screened by the compatibility collector; it is not authorization to read another
tenant/group. No real session can call unknown/missing routing 'successful nil'.

Redb point reads already inspect borrowed guards before copying admitted owners.
The same pattern can validate a catalog envelope/namespace/length/expiry and
return Omitted(stored_len) without copying/decoding the payload. This avoids a
user-owned payload buffer; it does NOT prove fewer disk reads or less engine
page/cache materialization. Zero-length stored data at max_bytes0 is Bytes,
not Missing or Omitted. Corrupt storage envelope/read failure differs from
valid stored bytes whose typed FSV2 payload later fails decoding. Reopen must
not depend on a live atom/export table or turn a context-dependent value into
storage corruption. The projector preserves the source's redacted error versus
missing/omitted rules and sticky fields. max_bytes is not a whole I/O/memory cap.

The current decoder consumes Vec. Stable fetch dedup therefore does NOT remove
copies needed for source per-occurrence decode. Admit a reached fallible copy
for each occurrence before moving it into the decoder, including actual retained
capacity under its profile; do not move the same unique owner repeatedly or
introduce a silent cache. Count ref/key/slot/unique-owner arrays, serialization
frames, existing command/postimage owners, temporary copies and decode scratch
with clear live ranges. Drop retired buffers when safe. A borrowed redb guard
cannot escape its transaction as an owned document, and no self-referential
read batch is acceptable. A future read-only MVCC session could avoid taking
the current exclusive apply writer for queries, but must capture scope/control/
rows in one snapshot; that new capability is not created by this review.

One source-proven caller-buffer opportunity merits a later named experiment:
the ALREADY selected redb4.4.0 commit7061f7e60d4573311c0636e161d417b93e9fd2b1
Table::insert_reserve accepts a length and returns a mutable in-place row guard.
I fetched/read official table.rs300..347/425..476 online, blob
8cb910f0d5b9fb01347e2d5c7978d82aa54dd9ef:
[pinned redb table API](https://github.com/cberner/redb/blob/7061f7e60d4573311c0636e161d417b93e9fd2b1/src/table.rs#L451).
It could let a future serializer fill the complete catalog frame plus conditional
escape directly from borrowed command bytes, avoiding a full caller-side escaped
Vec followed by another frame Vec. It REPLACES an existing key: immutable
version-key absence/collision and all scope/size/admission checks must precede
reservation, and any subsequent failure aborts the whole consumed transaction.
Internal engine allocation/copy/zeroing and failure bounds need their own source
review before reducing existing charges. The API's existence is no measurement,
allocation theorem, selected adoption or new dependency/security review result.

Actual backend calls vary with cache/page state and are not one-to-one with

### Iterative ownership/count/depth proof from actual source

Budget checks all five positive limits plus WHOLE input len AND capacity before
new scratch/arenas. One work account spans wrapper/census/build/context/fixup.
The raw branch retains unchanged original bytes and one Binary range, including
escape-empty; typed mode requires version/exact EOF. Scanner::take bounds the
span before admitting its declared handling and advancing. A missing/truncated
span does not falsely charge a nonexistent huge operand. No original buffer
is normalized or replaced; Float99 uses fixed local scratch.

Census and build are modes of the SAME driver. Every loop either completes,
admits a next encoded node, processes a paid continuation or emits a paid
synthetic STRING element. No recursive scanner, recursive input tree or enqueue
of all future children exists. One current sibling continuation per encoded
level is retained. begin_node checks encoded depth INCLUDING each LIST0 alias;
push_frame grows prospectively and child depth is checked on entry. STRING
synthetic cells consume output counts/work but no invented encoded nesting.
Known-count minimum checks may still allocate bounded scratch before a later
refusal; there is no all-invalid-input/no-allocation promise.

slots checks cumulative edge counts, admits initialization, and fills only
already reserved result space. emit checks the node bound/work before arena
append and build's expected count. write_edge checks a finished child ID and
admitted slot before mutation. Children complete before parent IDs: tuple/map/
list, foreign module/node/creator/free children and STRING head/tail nodes are
postorder. A zero-head alias reuses its completed tail ID without adding a graph
cycle. Build requires exact finished/edge/max-depth counts and exact EOF. Final
edge validation catches sentinel/unfilled IDs before map publication. Private
node ranges/IDs originate from checked input/counters, and all public views
remain checked. No external document constructor can introduce a cycle.

Census frames drop before exact node/edge reservations; immutable context and
bounded virtual function bindings intentionally survive into build, then drop
before returning. Parsing, equality and document destruction own only flat Vecs
of fixed metadata/ranges/IDs. Deep invalid input returns with flat scratch/partial
arena drop, not an input-depth-dependent recursive destructor. Fixed stack enum/
argument assembly and standard-library internals are not a whole-stack theorem.
I found no unbounded native recursive call or attacker-controlled allocating
leaf conversion in these paths; actual deep-profile execution remains separate.

### Equality, context and prospective resource inspection

Map validation walks postorder so maps used as keys are already unique. Iterative
comparison holds one actual candidate/continuation and no recursive call. Map
keys/values match independent of pair ordering; list cursors cross head ranges
and tail-list segments, preserving improper tails without materializing a new
list. Empty-list/atom nil and integer/float classes differ. Normalized small/big
integers, sign truthiness/padding/zero, STRING/LIST and Latin1/UTF8 atom aliases
are mathematical; finite floats retain distinct zero bits. Bit comparisons mask
unused last bits. No raw encoded-key comparator, normalized tree, random hash,
rank interner or shared mutable runtime is introduced. Pairwise/nested comparison
can be nonlinear; exhausted work is an explicit operational Limit.

Reached captured tables are validated once per decode, but every actual lookup,
row/digest/name comparison and normalization traversal is charged again. Name
validation/UTF8/character iteration has admitted lengths; equality does not
allocate normalized strings. Virtual function bindings use original input spans
plus fixed metadata. Full-entry/old-uniq collision profiles stay explicit rather
than guessing equality or invoking a function. Ordinary foreign PID/port/ref/
export/function DATA retains source ordering/guards and no execution capability.
I cross-checked pinned external.c dec_pid/port/reference/export/newfun ranges.
The extra official pinned export.h declarations confirm active-export arity is
unsigned, supporting the selected low32 conversion after the LP64 small-integer
guard (blob7a218f27b785cd5be2ccb403ee3d0ed5d340bd37):
[pinned export interface](https://github.com/erlang/otp/blob/aa5b1d3366762ff8f420f2aa6a52604b481530c9/erts/emulator/beam/export.h#L131).
Known unsupported native/local, zero-ref/empty-bit and function-collision lanes
are visible operational limits, not blanket unsupported common types or corrupt
data. No foreign execution bridge or full source-profile claim is implied.

reserve_exact first admits checked target*sizeof(T)+old_capacity*sizeof(T)+1,
then calls try_reserve_exact(target-LEN). Scratch grows in bounded batches only
when reached capacity is insufficient. Result pushes stay within the census
reservation; continuation/binding pushes stay within prospectively obtained
capacity. There is no refund or budget reset between passes/maps. Allocator
excess capacity and latency, compiler-elided loads, backend/native memory,
GC/RSS and fixed helper stack remain outside this logical operation theorem.
Parser pop+dispatch is one admitted continuation cohort; ARCH directly confirmed
that interpretation. It is not one unit per every Rust metadata peek/field read;
equality has its separately admitted concrete transitions. That qualification
DOES NOT excuse the distinct repeated node/edge omissions below.

Actual grammar/resource conclusions, communicated directly to ARCH/Root:
- Shape parsing has NO65535 or4096 limit. It recognizes f, canonical fa:0..255,
  or canonical43-character SHA-shaped base64url tag. It does not authenticate a
  digest's origin or partition placement. Regular p/r/e/s uses the LAST colon:
  the nonempty ID can itself contain colons, NUL and invalidUTF8. Versions are
  exact canonical decimal0..9007199254740991, not signs/leading zeroes/trimmed text.
- Named n requires exactly encoded-owner:encoded-name:version. Decoded owner/name
  must be nonempty; both encoded components must be canonical unpadded URL Base64.
  Return clearly named ORIGINAL ENCODED borrowed spans, not fake decoded slices.
  URL alphabet/length-remainder/canonical unused-bit validation needs no decoded
  Vec: remainder1 is impossible; remainder2 needs low4 zero bits, remainder3 low2.
  The fixed43-character digest needs the corresponding exact32-byte shape.
- The raw Keys integer renderers themselves also print negative/unbounded
  versions and regular empty IDs, which then FAIL the shape predicate. The new
  typed constructors therefore cover the explicitly selected CANONICAL ELIGIBLE
  subset, not alleged full raw-constructor parity. Version>MAX or empty mandatory
  fields is InvalidInput; checked arithmetic/excess output is operational Limit;
  actual std TryReserveError remains ReserveFailed. ValueStore's public owner/
  name4096 option cap is a distinct API and must not leak into this Keys-level
  parser/render profile. The renderer's separate limit is min(maximum,65535).
- Omitted value-key partition is Automatic=CRC32(ID)%256; named uses OWNER ID.
  It differs from Global={f}. Explicit canonical __flow_auto__:0..255 selects
  that bucket; malformed auto-looking bytes are SHA256-hashed WHOLE, not rejected
  or coerced. Empty Explicit binary is the actual SHA(empty) tag branch, distinct
  from Global. All IDs/names/partitions are opaque binary bytes; no trim/UTF8 rule.

[erlang.erl](https://github.com/erlang/otp/blob/aa5b1d3366762ff8f420f2aa6a52604b481530c9/erts/preloaded/src/erlang.erl)
blobcfb724920adece4b1c9d1f2ffcb0a9f41d19651f documents IEEE802.3 CRC32.
The pinned bundled
[crc32.c](https://github.com/erlang/otp/blob/aa5b1d3366762ff8f420f2aa6a52604b481530c9/erts/emulator/zlib/crc32.c)
blob6c38f5c04c6a87e711ecb50b668e17131fa8fae2 shows reflected polynomial
0xedb88320 and pre/post complement. This is NOT CRC32C or architecture-dependent
random hashing. Current official OTP28 docs corroborate the checksum family;
the pinned source, not the newer doc release, binds this operand. Independent
literal source VM goldens for empty and ID123456789 remain Root's validation,
not an experiment or passing source test performed by this reviewer.

Prospective allocation/work design is appropriate within the selected roster.
Compute Base64 output using checked quotient/remainder arithmetic rather than
unchecked4*(n+2)/3. Validate semantic fields before measured output admission,
then ONE fallible final-key Vec reservation for the exact logical result length.
Write owner/name Base64 directly to that final buffer, with checked destination
ranges and a fixed decimal scratch; do not allocate intermediate encoded-owner,
encoded-name, tag, format String or escaped-value Vec. If the chosen safe writer
zero-initializes the result before filling it, that is an additional real byte
pass, not a claimed removed allocation. Actual Vec capacity can exceed requested
length; no capacity-equality assertion, shrink/Box conversion or fabricated OOM
probe is justified. Real reserve-error propagation is reviewable without claiming
that small bounded key tests exercise allocator failure.

CRC work is proportional to ID length; SHA to WHOLE explicit partition length;
Base64/copy/zero-fill to their actual input/output lengths. A bounded49-byte SHA
tag does not bound the borrowed partition's input bytes or acquisition cost.
Checked output caps may reject before unnecessary hashing, but do not mint an
ingress/work budget. The allocation-free shape parser still examines caller-
provided bytes; avoiding source decode/reencode temporaries does not prove total
CPU, nativeR, allocator behavior or measured speed. Slice views can retain a much
larger caller backing. Caller ownership/admission remains a real later boundary.

Support's logical binary encoding needs only a borrowed two-part description:
if and ONLY if input STARTS_WITH b"FSV2", prefix it with static b"FSV2\x01";
otherwise prefix is empty; second slice is the ENTIRE unchanged input. Stored
length is input.len() or checked input.len()+5, compared to the supplied maximum.
There is NO reference65535 limit here. max0/empty succeeds; it is not an invalid
limits value. Exact FSV2, unknown modes, type2-looking ETF and already escaped-
looking logical input must all be escaped ONCE rather than trusted/reinterpreted.
The two borrowed parts and usize result need no joined buffer or source decode.
Their lifetime belongs to input backing, not a temporary slice descriptor. A
nonempty pointer/content witness after that descriptor scope is meaningful;
empty cases alone cannot establish the borrowing property. Later storage will
still own/serialize its real consumed output, so this is not a whole zero-copy
publication/transaction claim.

Both builders reject invalid mandatory components/version FIRST, including at
max0. Regular fixed bytes are checked ID length+decimal digits+6; named fixed
bytes are checked encoded-owner+encoded-name+decimal digits+7. They enforce the
separate min(caller,65535) output maximum, subtract fixed bytes BEFORE tag work,
then pass actual remaining space to the selected four-argument render_tag.
The wider raw Keys integer/empty-component renderings and4096 ValueStore public
option cap remain explicitly outside these eligible constructor decisions.

The actual tag worker implements Global5, Automatic8..10 and hashed49 COMPLETE
prefix bytes. Automatic refuses remaining<8 BEFORE CRC, then checks the actual
one/two/three-digit bucket length BEFORE writing. This preserves the frozen auto
25-success/24-Limit oracle instead of pessimistically requiring10 prefix bytes.
Explicit canonical auto keys select their bucket; malformed auto-looking binary
keys fall through to SHA of the WHOLE original bytes. Global checks5 before copy;
hash checks49 BEFORE Sha256::digest. Empty Explicit hashes, distinct from Global.
The reflected CRC uses0xedb88320, initial all-ones and final complement; explicit
low-byte extraction is endian-independent modulo256. Named Automatic uses the
raw OWNER bytes, not their encoded spelling. SHA writes exactly32-byte digest's
43-character canonical URL representation into fixed49 scratch. No dynamic tag
or intermediate encoded owner/name buffer is created.

Each successful builder invokes ONE reserve_key on its exact logical final size.
That helper checks the source cap, creates one empty Vec, calls actual
try_reserve_exact(length), propagates the genuine TryReserveError, then resize
zero-fills length. Because reservation guarantees capacity>=length, this resize
requires no second growth. The zero-fill is a REAL additional byte pass, followed
by checked final content writes; it is not hidden as a removed cost. put checks
cursor addition and destination ranges; named B64 encodes DIRECTLY into its
measured final intervals and checks returned counts; final cursors must match.
The B64 quotient/remainder length calculation is checked before allocation/copy.
Fixed decimal scratch is20bytes; bounded u64 digits and checked cursor movement
avoid panic or string formatting. All untrusted slicing uses checked ranges;
there is no recursion or recursively owned output tree/drop.

This proves the one explicit fallible KEY-owner reserve in these wrappers, not
that the entire allocator/dependency/engine runs once or returns exact capacity.
sha2's selected digest is an existing permitted dependency, not a newly measured
allocation/CPU backend. No fake OOM/capacity==requested assertion, Vec->Box swap,

CRC/SHA may inspect the whole supplied ID/partition; bounded output does not cap

remain real work. Caller acquisition/input/work authority is still external.

otherwise original len, then compare caller maximum without a65535 reference cap.
parts first performs that check, chooses static FSV2+1 or empty prefix and returns

### Concrete variable-byte proof and forbidden additional passes

Let E be decoded.encoded().len(), n the actual represented source item count,
and L the sum BY OCCURRENCE of each item's partition length, ID length, and
lengths of only its actually SUPPLIED payload/result/error operands. V5/V6
encode.rs64..82 writes each pair of IDs and operation fields explicitly. The
public codec163..164 and219..312 writes the same raw pair and explicit optional
values. V7 codec143..154 writes the singleton pair and optional payload; its
materializer229..244 copies those exact operands. Reader.raw46..51 reads one
u32-length-delimited span, and Writer.raw84..89 writes that span literally.
Therefore L<=E in each supported validated profile. Identical source bytes in
two items count twice in L AND occur twice in E; shared content does not break
the bound. No compression, base64 expansion or external value resolution occurs
in these selected command fields.

A source/postimage equality must first pay its fixed shape/length test. On unequal
lengths it may reject without traversing the larger old owner. On equal lengths,
pay both byte spans prospectively before equality. One comparison of each pair
per pass costs at most2L; the complete count+emission traversal costs at most4L,
which is <=4E. Some(empty) has zero byte length but still has real presence/shape
visits and must retain a true supplied bit. Equal supplied replacement is still
supplied, independent of its predecessor's bytes. Omitted values require no old
payload/result/error scan.

Do not recompute a whole command digest: decoded.digest() is already cached, and
EntryIdentity::prepared supplies a fixed32-byte value. One association comparison
can conservatively admit32+32=64 examined bytes. Do not re-encode or rehash E in
each walk/check helper. Do not recompute HistoryEntry.event_id, compare entire
WorkflowRecord/ScopedPlan values via derived Eq, inspect old omitted value blobs,
rerun reducers, walk predecessor state/history strings, derive keys/refs, or
perform a nested search for each source index. Those add work unrelated to the
listed raw operands or repeat L beyond two passes, so the proposed envelope does
not prove them funded. HistoryEntry itself has no second variable partition/ID
copy: scalar kind/version/outcome agreement suffices for this provenance slice.
The source's already executed reducer/retention/response work remains in its
existing admission contract rather than being falsely counted as the new scan.

### Proposed reached-event ledger within64+64*n+4E

The following is a concrete CONDITIONAL design budget for the unimplemented
roster, not a claim the later source automatically meets it. Each listed amount
is an upper slot count on one item's path in ONE pass:

| Reached fixed event category | Maximum slots |
| --- | ---: |
| Item visit, result classification, loop continuation | 3 |
| Supported operation/presence dispatch and checked source-index conversion | 2 |
| Outcome, write and history shape visits | 3 |
| Kind/version/terminal/shape-consistency validations | 4 |
| Partition and ID equality length guards | 2 |
| Source and postimage Option visits for three value kinds | 6 |
| Numeric descriptor construction OR attached-descriptor validation | 1 |
| Checked emitted-count advance | 1 |
| Prospective output slot/capacity check | 1 |
| Output push | 1 |
| Total upper bound per item per pass | 24 |

A concrete future implementation may use fewer events, including no push in the
count pass. Each reached validation, result/no-op classification and continuation
must be admitted before it executes; a skipped branch is not falsely billed as
reached work. Do not use this table to hide a second real validation/visit inside
a label after source inspection reveals it. The literal future code census must
show how every reached event maps to a slot, or revise the bound/algorithm before
acceptance. This is a logical high-level tariff, not one unit per Rust field
access, native instruction, memory allocation, stack word or physical CPU cycle.

Reserve at most16 further call-wide fixed slots: account/cap setup; checked bound
arithmetic; supported profile; root representation; actual count; result-count
association; next-entry presence; digest shape; attachment lane; completed
semantic-count check; checked real-R multiplication; R-cap comparison; actual
workspace comparison; one reserve transition; final emitted/attached count
agreement; owner-return state. Count only those actually reached. These fixed
operations must not contain hidden variable scans. Besides them the fixed digest
byte comparison costs<=64. Supported frames have n>=1: common::count144..153
rejects0 and V1/V7 are singleton. A complete two-pass ceiling is consequently

  64 +16 +2*24*n +4L <=64 +64*n +4E.

The16n margin pays the16 call-wide events even for n1; it is not permission to
spend an unbounded remainder or reclaim/refund already spent work. Both passes,
all helper calls and reached comparisons use ONE monotonically increasing
Charge account. Checked additions/multiplications refuse overflow. Preflight
may prove that the caller's maximum_work admits the envelope, but it must not
reset the used count before emission or before check helpers. The inspector's
one source/attached verification traversal can fit the stronger ceiling without
allocating or rebuilding the owner. A separately invoked inspector has its own
explicit caller work cap; earlier producer work is not free credit for that call.

The selected u32 conversion is reached once per real effect row PER PASS, before
construction/validation, not once for the batch and not an unchecked cast. The
source count is u32-framed and admitted<=100000; indices remain0..n-1, including
holes. Later descriptor-driven access needs a checked usize conversion and a
bounds-checked lookup. That is another reached fixed validation if the chosen
code uses it, not an uncharged linear source search. No extra index Vec is needed.

Walk independent.results().enumerate() before filtering. A real Err and a
successful no-op both have reached item/result/continuation work, in BOTH passes
if a second pass is reached because other rows survive. Neither emits a row.
Do not use final_indices() or history_plans() to recover source positions: the
former loses intermediate histories, the latter loses error/no-op holes. Every
real write/history, including ExtendLease's zero supplied mask, emits its original
index/version. Root's source correction is incorporated: repeated Cancel after
Cancel is a real terminal-record error; the separate repeated Complete against
completed state is the genuine no-history case. This changes no SUT claim or
workload evidence. StepContinue/Claim/Reclaim remain unsupported in V5/V6;
StartAndClaim is only supported in V7. Generic value_bytes zero slots do not
make an unsupported operation an admitted carry row. Cancel's slot0 is ERROR,
Complete slot1 is RESULT, Fail/Retry slot1 is ERROR; do not infer kind from the
flattened ValueRow built by fresh::stage_values.

### Real R after semantic count, F on every returned plan

Complete immutable semantic/count validation precedes result allocation. Only
then calculate R=checked(real_row_count*size_of::<actual Effect>()). The public
maximum_effect_bytes caps R. bounds2 may calculate n*size_of as an arithmetic
upper estimate, but must NOT enforce that estimate against the public cap before
the semantic pass: all-no-history None has realR0 and no Vec allocation, so cap0
can succeed with adequate association/work checks. Zero-mask REAL history has
realR>0 and cannot be silently returned as None. No temporary collector/map/set,
payload clone, per-item Box, shrink or Vec-to-Box conversion is warranted.

F=size_of::<Option<actual Effects>>() is separate fixed resident metadata on EVERY
returned plan, even None/replay/conflict/expired/whole rejection. Count F once;
the Option already includes its Vec-owner representation. Neither assumed NPO
nor old512/531/192n slack owns it. Use actual compiled size_of later, not an
invented24/16-byte row or a layout/probe claim. One final try_reserve_exact for
real rows is fallible, after semantic checks and prospective coexistence; normal
allocator overprovision is not capacity==requested or a total physical bound.
ReserveFailed is operational; no fake OOM or exercised-failure claim is made.

I rederived the four actual Fresh coexistence sites. Let B be captured decision
bytes, A caller additional_live, H actual retained header byte lengths and S the
measured response size. decoder_bound305..315 is4E+B+1024n+2048; inputs157..163
adds192n+256+B+A. Thus keep the existing conservative early owner bound
4E+2B+1216n+2304+A and add F+R BEFORE the new reserve. After the accepted decision
and before decoded destruction, build the numeric owner while those source fields
remain available. Release operation, isolation, state_keys, claim_filters in the
current order; do not retain decoded value Vecs through response construction.
Late V5 phase221..227 is E+B+H+531+A; V6 independent131..141, V1 public127..137
and V7 atomic121..131 are E+B+H+512+S+A. Add the SAME retained F+R to each before

### Official Rust optimization research; bounded post-STOP checklist

Root requested this primary-source refresh while the sole BODY author works.
I read current official documentation online, not the changing implementation.
The std pages identify1.99.1/2026-10-13; these stable guarantees are guidance for
the frozen1.99.0 toolchain, not a compiler upgrade or an actual layout result.
No source/probe/file/API/roster/dependency change or workload is authorized here.

[Vec::try_reserve_exact](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#method.try_reserve_exact)
provides enough additional capacity on success and reports capacity/allocation
failure, but the allocator may overprovide. The
[Vec guarantees](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#guarantees)
say individual pushes within reported capacity do not reallocate. This supports
the selected counted row spine, not capacity==requested or a total heap bound.
[Vec::into_boxed_slice](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#method.into_boxed_slice)
consumes its owner and removes spare capacity; the linked
[shrink_to_fit](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#method.shrink_to_fit)
behavior can reallocate. Therefore a Vec-to-Box conversion is not inherently a
zero-copy win, and is not selected. Vec cloning creates another owned collection;
moving the existing numeric owner is the intended lifecycle.

[std::hint::black_box](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/hint/fn.black_box.html)
is a best-effort optimizer barrier for benchmark inputs/results. It is not a
correctness, work-account, security or constant-time guarantee. The official
[benchmark guidance](https://doc.rust-lang.org/unstable-book/library-features/test.html)
recommends isolating intended timed work, repeatable iteration state and keeping
setup separate; I use those methodology principles, not its unstable test API.
[Cargo bench](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/commands/cargo-bench.html) normally
uses an optimized benchmark profile and allows custom harnesses. Actual settings
may be overridden; [Cargo profiles](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/profiles.html)
also cautions that a higher optimization level need not be faster. No new bench,
nightly feature, dependency or profile is proposed.

   k rows, with a prospective capacity check before each push. k0 checks F then

2. Follow that SAME Vec into the plan before donors drop, and through all four

3. Recount every reached item/result/continuation/descriptor/index conversion in

   Use one monotonic account; no reset/refund, whole omitted-payload scan,
   rehash/reducer rerun or uncounted third pass. Revalidate the proposed64+64n+4E
   ceiling against actual stopped source, including new private context errors.

### Actual symbolic census, including the required corrections

Let a be the number of original source Optional value fields examined in the
selected operation arm (0..2), s the number actually supplied (0..a), and Li the
item's partition+ID+supplied-value byte lengths BY OCCURRENCE. A's value_effect
has18+s fixed charged units, plus2Li byte units on a real-row path. The required
source extraction adds a; the final map/terminal adds4. Corrected fixed Step is
therefore22+a+s<=26, NOT the speculative24-item table. A no-op exits earlier;
independent Err skips Step after its paid result classification. No skipped
branch is reported as reached work.

With the output Option fee, atomic walk overhead is5 per real row in the count
pass and7 in emission. Independent walk is6/8 because it separately visits the
Result. Thus two complete real-row passes cost at most64n atomic or66n
independent, excluding compared bytes. Error/no-op prefixes are lower, but keep
the original source indices and every reached result/continuation visit.

The exact corrected successful-builder call-wide debit is85:
initial1 + entry validation68 (including fixed digest64) + first walk header3
+ post-count R/base/attempt/compare/zero5 + remaining subtraction1
+ reserve-helper3 + second walk header3 + final emitted-count comparison1.
This includes the private bridge's real-R arithmetic and reserve helper; the
new result alias adds no dynamic traversal. Successful inspector with attached
independent rows is at most78+33n+2L, with atomic32n; fixed78 is initial1+
entry68+attachment1+check header3+final count1+four footer checks4. Missing-owner,
None, rejection and early-error paths terminate earlier. This is a selected
high-level logical tariff, not accounting for every Rust metadata load, the
work controller's own recursive checks, machine instruction, allocator call or
physical CPU/stack/RSS cost.

As proved from the actual codecs in the preceding preparation, L=sum Li<=E.
Matched lengths are checked BEFORE examining either byte span. Each source/
postimage pair is compared once per pass after spending BOTH lengths; the two
passes use<=4L and the inspector<=2L. There is no old omitted payload traversal,
whole-record Eq, event-ID parsing/reformatting, SHA/digest recomputation, reducer
rerun, lookup rescan, temporary map or third semantic/count traversal.

Consequently a corrected builder's explicit ceiling is85+66n+4L. To retain a
64 base and prove64+K*n+4E with ONLY L<=E and n>=1, K must be at least87 at n1.
A plain80 item coefficient is insufficient for this proof. I recommended the
simple96 item coefficient, and Root agreed:85+66n+4L<=64+96n+4E because
30n-21>=9 for n>=1. This uses NO unpriced frame-byte slack. It conservatively
bounds already billed events; it does not make missing visits free. Inspector

[Box constructors](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/boxed/struct.Box.html#method.new) allocate for non-zero-sized payloads and return Box directly. [Box::try_new](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/boxed/struct.Box.html#method.try_new) is still marked nightly allocator_api. Therefore a speculative Known(Some(Box<Binding>)) would add a real ownership/allocation boundary; its ordinary constructor is not a recoverable allocation Result. [Vec reservation/conversion](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#method.try_reserve_exact) documents recoverable reserve errors but permits allocator overprovision. [into_boxed_slice](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#method.into_boxed_slice) removes spare capacity using shrink behavior; shrinking can reallocate. Thus neither capacity==requested nor an always allocation-free/fallible Vec-to-Box workaround follows. [Cargo bench](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/commands/cargo-bench.html) uses an optimized bench profile by default; libtest's bench attribute remains unstable and custom harness selection matters. Current official primary learning was ACTUALLY read, not inferred from search snippets. Cargo pins redb4.4.0/git7061f7e60d4573311c0636e161d417b93e9fd2b1. Online latest pages identify4.4.0; the version-qualified URLs initially failed, then official latest succeeded. Exact cached transactions.rs SHA4dee5b29 and table.rs eabcd7e2 were read at the relevant API bodies:

- [ReadTransaction](https://docs.rs/redb/latest/redb/struct.ReadTransaction.html) and [Database close semantics](https://docs.rs/redb/latest/redb/struct.Database.html#close-semantics): read tables retain their snapshot guard; a private context need not be self-referential. Database drop can invalidate outstanding reads, so the frozen returned Vec data still must be owned. Keep snapshot handles short-lived; this is no page-cache/RSS bound.
- [Table::insert](https://docs.rs/redb/latest/redb/struct.Table.html#method.insert) replaces existing keys. Generic publication::put therefore supplies neither immutability nor alias authority. Check immutable identity/exact bytes+digest under the same writer, and authorize alias rebinding separately. Drop read guards before mutable table use; do not clone entire old rows just to satisfy borrow lifetime. insert_reserve/in-place conversion is NOT selected or a claimed optimization gain.
- [WriteTransaction::commit](https://docs.rs/redb/latest/redb/struct.WriteTransaction.html#method.commit) and [Immediate durability](https://docs.rs/redb/latest/redb/enum.Durability.html): retain consumed atomic commit with Immediate. Non-poison commit failures may already be durable; preserve Uncertain/fence/reopen, not a universal no-change assertion after commit. Errors/refusals before commit still abort and must preserve frozen no-publication controls.
- [Rust File sync/drop behavior](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/fs/struct.File.html#method.sync_all): closing is not the explicit synchronization/error boundary. The pinned optimized/fallback backend delegates sync_data to File; existing path create syncs the parent directory, but database-taking constructors explicitly leave cache/file/directory setup to the caller. Library durability semantics do not establish this product's unexecuted Linux/macOS power-failure/native profile. No change to sync mode for speed is recommended.
